Let's write helpers:
```
def _safe_eval(seq):
    try:
        s = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if not isinstance(s, float):
        try: s = float(s)
        except: return float('inf')
    if not math.isfinite(s): return float('inf')
    return s
```
`evaluate_sequence` returns np.inf for invalid; float.

```

b = float(base); r=float(rel)
if invalid: return None

v = b * factor
if not math.isfinite(v) or v <0 or v>1000: return None
if v == b: return None
seq=[b]*N
seq[pos]=float(v)
return seq
```

```
def _relative_values(count, min_log, max_log):
    vals = []
    if count <=0: return vals
    if min_log == max_log: vals.append(10.0**min_log)
    else:
       for e in np.linspace(min_log, max_log, count): vals.append(float(10.0**e))
    # also add powers? 
    return vals
```

We can implement objective:
```
def fast_score(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a,float)
    S = a.sum()
    pad = 1 << ((2*n-2).bit_length())  # power of two
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, pad)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, pad)[:2*n-1]
    maxb = c.max()
    return 2*n*maxb/(S*S)
```
For real nonnegative, small negative roundoff possible; max might be slightly negative? but positive. Use `np.maximum(0,c)`.

We can also use the LP Frank-Wolfe more directly: Given active weights w (duality), the linear minimization of ∑d_i (z_i - a_i) over simplex chooses vertex j = argmin d_i. Update a ← (1-t)a + t e_j. We can combine:
- "coordinate boosting": find indices with low d, add mass there; indices high d remove. Use normalized softmax over -d: target b_i ∝ exp(-β d_i). Update a = (1-α)a + α b. Pseudo iteration:
```
a = normalize(best)
for it:
  c = conv(a)
  score = 2n*max(c)/1
  weights = (c + eps)^p
  weights /= weights.sum()
  d = 2*corr(weights, a)  # length n
  # target distribution proportional to exp(-beta * d / d_scale)
  d_norm = (d - d.min())/(d.max()-d.min()+eps)
  target = np.exp(-beta*d_norm)
  target /= target.sum()
  a_new = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*target
  improve? (if score lower)
```
But target uniform if d range small? okay.

However the gradient of max c is d = 2corr(w,a); increasing a where d small reduces active c? For each active c_k, derivative wrt a_i =2a_{k-i}. If we increase a where derivative small, active constraints increase least. This is Frank-Wolfe linear minimize. Using softmax over -d is reasonable.

We need normalize sum=1; d scale can be set to 2 max weights? Use `d_scale = np.max(d)` or adaptive.

We can also update multiplicatively: `a_new = a * np.exp(-eta * (d - d.mean()))` then normalize, which preserves zeros and less extreme. Since a positive. This might be more stable. Use `eta` small. Combine with occasional target simplex updates.

A. Multiplicative active-weight update:
```
def _optimize_multiplicative(a0, n, p, steps, eta, rng):
   a=a0.copy()
   a/=a.sum(); best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score
   for it in range(steps):
      c=conv(a)
      maxc=c.max()
      score=2*n*maxc
      if score<best_score: update
      # weights
      cw = c + 1e-300
      w = cw ** p
      wsum=w.sum()
      if wsum>0: w/=wsum
      d = _corr_weighted(w,a) # 2*... maybe without 2
      # scale d to log-likelihood stability
      d_shifted = d - np.min(d)
      d_scale = d_shifted.max()+eps
      eta_eff = eta / (1.0 + it/anneal?) 
      factor = np.exp(-eta_eff * d_shifted / d_scale)
      # maybe add uniform component to avoid zeros
      a = a * factor
      a = a / a.sum()
      # occasional random restart if stuck
   return best
```

def _active_gradient(a, w, pad):
    # w length 2n-1, a length n
    # compute corr_i = sum_{k=i}^{i+n-1?} w_k a_{k-i}; use convolution with reversed a
    a_rev = a[::-1]
    c = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(w, pad)*np.fft.rfft(a_rev, pad), pad)

Let's design iterative loops with time checks:
```
def _optimize_one(a0, n, max_time, seed, mode):
    a = normalize(a0)
    pad = 1 << (3*n-3).bit_length()
    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a,pad)
    p_initial=2; p_final=32
    eta=0.05
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time>break
       c=conv_a(a,pad)
       maxc=c.max(); score=2*n*maxc # sum=1
       if score<best_score: best=a.copy(); best_score=score
       logc=np.log(np.maximum(c,1e-300))
       # p schedule: start low then high
       p = p_initial + (p_final-p_initial)*(it/total)^2
       w=np.exp(p*(logc - logc.max())); w/=w.sum()
       d=active_grad(a,w,pad)
       dmax=d.max(); dmin=d.min()
       if dmax>dmin:
           s=d-dmin; scale=max(s.max(),1e-12)
       else scale=1
       # try different step sizes
       for eta_try in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25):
           factor=np.exp(-eta_try*s/scale)
           a_new=a*factor
           sumn=a_new.sum()
           if sumn<=0 or not finite: continue
           a_new/=sumn
           score_new=fast_score(a_new,pad) # can reuse? not
           if score_new < best_score: update; a=a_new; eta=eta_try; break
           # if no improve, maybe still accept slightly with simulated annealing? no
       else:
           # if no accepted, reduce eta and maybe do convex target
           eta *= 0.95
           # occasional target update
       # mix uniform tiny
       a = a + 1e-8/n; a/=a.sum()
    return best,best_score
```
But computing score_new inside eta loop requires extra convolutions (3 FFTs) per try, expensive. We can just take a step and if score worsens revert. Use small fixed eta and line search once? Maybe:
- Make a_new = a * exp(-eta s/scale). Evaluate. If improves, accept; else try half eta. If still not, reduce eta. This is typical.

But objective changes quadratically; small eta slow. Maybe use adaptive target update: after computing w, instead of tiny multiplicative, do a Frank-Wolfe convex combination with target b = softmax(-β d). β maybe 1/scale. This can change significantly. Let's test conceptually. If d high early low late, b shifts late. Update a=(1-α)a+α b, α decaying (e.g., 0.2). This is guaranteed to stay in simplex and may make progress even if gradient small. The linear minimization of gradient would choose argmin d; softmax is a smoothed minimizer. Use β to control. This may be more effective than multiplicative for making max flat.

Let's first design `_optimize_prefix(n, time_limit)` that returns best sequence/score. It can use a set of initial guesses and multiple optimizer modes.

Detailed optimizer:
```
def _optimize_sequence(init, n, time_limit, rng):
    a = np.clip(init,0,None).astype(float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return init? 
    a/=s

       a=best.copy()

       eta = 0.1

          if score<best_score: best=a; best_score=score; stuck=0; p maybe increase
          else: stuck++
          if stuck>some and not improve: p*=1.2; eta*=0.9
          # weights
          logc = np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300))
          w = np.exp(p*(logc-logc.max())); w /= w.sum()
          d = active_grad(a,w,pad)
          # normalize d
          dmean=d.mean()
          dstd=d.std()
          if dstd>0: z=(d-dmean)/dstd; else z=zeros
          # multiplicative update
          factor = np.exp(-eta * (z + 1.0?))? If use z, d high positive reduce. To keep mean factor 1 maybe use z. If z mean 0, product factor ~1 but some >1.
          a_new = a * np.exp(-eta * z)
          a_new += 1e-12
          a_new /= a_new.sum()
          if not finite: skip
          score_new=fast_score(a_new)
          if score_new < score or score_new < best_score + tol:
              # maybe accept if not worse than current score? For descent require <score.
              if score_new < score:
                  a=a_new; score=score_new; eta *=1.05
              else:
                  # try half eta
          else:
              eta *=0.9
          # every 50 iterations, FW update:
          beta = 1.0 (relative to z scale)
          b = np.exp(-eta_fw * z); b/=b.sum()
          a_fw = (1-alpha_fw)*a + alpha_fw*b
          if fast_score(a_fw)<score: accept
    return best
```

def _local_block_search(a,n,pad,iterations,rng):
   best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score
   for _ in range(iter):
      width = rng.integers(max(2,n//512), n//32)
      start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
      factor = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1))
      cand=best.copy(); cand[start:start+width]*=factor
      cand += 1e-12; cand/=cand.sum()
      score=fast_score(cand)
      if score<best_score: update
   return best
```

Optimization update details refined:
```
def _optimize_start(a0, n, time_limit, rng):
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return None...
    a /= s
    pad = _pad_length(n)
    best = a.copy()
    best_c = _fast_conv(a, pad)
    best_score = 2.0 * n * best_c.max()
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    p = 1.0
    eta = 0.2
    stuck = 0
    it = 0
    # number of accepted required to increase p
    while time.monotonic() < time_limit:
        c = _fast_conv(cur, pad)
        score = 2.0 * n * c.max()
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = cur.copy()
            stuck = 0
            # maybe increase p slightly to focus
            p = min(64.0, p * 1.01)
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.02)
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck > 50:
                p = min(64.0, p * 1.2)
                eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.9)
            if stuck > 500:
                # random restart from best with perturbation
                cur = best.copy()
                cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n))
                cur = np.maximum(cur, 0.0)
                cs = cur.sum()
                if cs <= 0: cur = best.copy()
                else: cur /= cs
                cur_score = best_score
                stuck = 0
                p = 1.0
                eta = 0.2
        # compute active weights
        logc = np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300))
        maxlog = logc.max()
        w = np.exp(p * (logc - maxlog))
        wsum = w.sum()
        if not finite or wsum<=0: w = np.full_like(c, 1.0/(2*n-1))
        else: w /= wsum
        d = _active_gradient(cur, w, pad, n)
        dmin = d.min(); dmax = d.max()
        denom = dmax - dmin
        if denom <= 1e-15:
            # next small perturb
            cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.01,size=n)); ... continue
        frac = 1.0 - eta * (d - dmin) / denom
        frac = np.clip(frac, 1e-6, None)
        new = cur * frac
        new += 1e-12
        ns = new.sum()
        if ns <=0 or not finite: ...
        new /= ns
        new_c = _fast_conv(new, pad)
        new_score = 2.0 * n * new_c.max()
        if new_score < cur_score or new_score < best_score:
            cur = new
            cur_score = new_score
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.01)
            it +=1
        else:
            # try smaller eta once
            frac = 1.0 - eta * 0.5 * (d - dmin) / denom
            frac = np.clip(frac,1e-6,None)
            new2 = cur*frac + 1e-12; new2/=new2.sum()
            new2_c = _fast_conv(new2,pad); new2_score=...
            if new2_score < cur_score:
                cur=new2; cur_score=new2_score; eta=max(0.02, eta*0.8)
            else:
                eta=max(0.02, eta*0.8)
                # if eta too small, maybe accept stochastic small change
                if eta < 0.03:
                   cur = best.copy(); cur *= exp(normal(0,0.02)); normalize; cur_score=best_score; stuck=0; p=1.0; eta=0.2
        it +=1
    return best, best_score
```
We call `_fast_conv` twice per iteration (current and new). We could reuse current c for active weights. For new score, need c_new. This is 2 conv calls. Each conv 2 FFT? Actually `_fast_conv` does rfft+irfft (2 transforms). Active gradient does 3 transforms. So current iteration: c conv (2), d (3), new conv (2) =7 transforms + if half another 2. For n=4498 pad=16384, maybe 0.001s? 7k iterations maybe many seconds. We can reduce new score evaluation by using gradient approximation? But exact objective needed. Could evaluate new score every M iterations? For descent, maybe update and next iteration computes score. We can avoid separate new conv: just set cur=new, and at top of next loop compute score. But if new bad, we can't revert unless keep old. We can keep old and after next score if worse than old, revert? That may oscillate. Simpler: accept unconditionally (cur=new) and track best; if it goes bad, restart from best. Then no separate new score. This halves transforms. Let's do:
```
# after direction, cur = new (unconditional but safe)
# at next iteration compute score. If score much worse than current best, maybe revert if > best*(1+0.1)
```

```
def _as_nonnegative_float_array(seq):
    try: a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)
    except: return None
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return None
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0); a=np.minimum(a,1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return None
    return a
```

`_normalize(a, target=1.0)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float).copy(); a=np.maximum(a,0)
s=a.sum()
if s<=1e-300: return None
return a*(target/s)
```

`_prefix_sequence(n)` etc.

`_pad_length(n)`:
```
m = max(1, 3*n - 3)
p = 1
while p < m: p <<=1
return p
```
Using bit_length faster:
```
return 1 << max(0, (m - 1).bit_length())
```
For m=1, (0).bit_length=0 =>1.

`_fast_conv(a, pad)`:
```
fa=np.fft.rfft(a, pad)
c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, pad)[:2*a.size-1]
# remove negative tiny
c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
return c
```
If `a.size` 1, pad 1, `irfft` length 1, slice okay? rfft length 1, square, irfft length 1. Good.

`_score_fast(a, pad)`:
```
c=_fast_conv(a,pad)
m=c.max()
if not finite: return inf
return 2.0*len(a)*m
```

`_active_gradient(a, w, pad, n)`:
```
ar = a[::-1]
Ww=np.fft.rfft(w, pad)
Wa=np.fft.rfft(ar, pad)
cor=np.fft.irfft(Ww*Wa, pad)
return cor[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Need ensure length cor at least 3n-2; pad≥3n-3. Good.

Pseudo code:
```
def _optimize_start(a0, n, time_limit, rng):
    a = _normalize(a0,1.0)
    if a is None:
       a = _prefix_sequence(n); a=_normalize(a,1.0)
    pad = _pad_length(n)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = _score_fast(a,pad)
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    eta = 0.25
    p = 2.0
    last_imp = 0
    it = 0
    end = time.monotonic()+time_limit
    # maybe initial random perturbinations? no
    while time.monotonic() < end:
       c = _fast_conv(cur, pad)
       score = 2.0*n*c.max()
       if not finite: cur=best.copy(); continue
       if score < best_score:
          best_score = score; best = cur.copy(); last_imp=it; eta=min(1.0, eta*1.03)
       if score > best_score * 1.5:
          # too bad, revert with small perturb
          cur = best.copy()
          cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,size=n))
          cur += 1e-9; cur/=cur.sum()
          score = best_score
       # active weights
       logc = np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300))
       maxlog=logc.max()
       w = np.exp(p*(logc - maxlog))
       wsum = w.sum()
       if not finite or wsum <=0:
          w = np.ones(c.size)/c.size
       else: w/=wsum
       d = _active_gradient(cur, w, pad, n)
       dmin=d.min(); dmax=d.max(); denom=dmax-dmin
       if denom > 1e-15:
          frac = 1.0 - eta*(d-dmin)/denom
          frac = np.clip(frac, 1e-8, None)
          new = cur*frac
       else:
          # random small multiple
          new = cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.02,size=n))
       new += 1e-10
       ns = new.sum()
       if not finite or ns<=0:
          new = cur.copy()
       else:
          new /= ns
       # occasionally FW-style aggressive update every 20 iterations
       if it % 25 == 20 and denom > 1e-15:
          z = (d-dmin)/denom
          beta = 1.0
          b = np.exp(-beta*z)
          bsum=b.sum()
          if finite and bsum>0:
             b/=bsum
             alpha = 0.05 + 0.05*(eta)
             cand = (1-alpha)*new + alpha*b
             cs=cand.sum(); if cs>0: cand/=cs; new=cand
       cur = new
       it +=1
       # adapt p/eta
       if it - last_imp > 100:
          eta = max(0.05, eta*0.95)
          p = min(64.0, p*1.05)
       elif it - last_imp > 300:
          # restart from best with larger perturbation
          cur = best.copy()
          cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,size=n))
          cur += 1e-8; cur/=cur.sum()
          eta=0.25; p=2.0; last_imp=0
       else:
          # slowly increase p if improving
          p = min(64.0, p*1.002)
    return best, best_score
```
This uses current score computed each iter, no line search. Could wander. Revert if >1.5*best. Good.

Let's write helper `_trial_block_perturb(best, n, pad, rng)` used in optimizer. But to avoid too many helper? okay top-level.

```
def _block_perturb_candidate(best, n, pad, rng):
    cand = best.copy()
    width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n // 1000), max(3, n // 64)))
    if width >= n: width = max(1,n//2)
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
    factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.15)))
    cand[start:start+width] *= factor
    cand += 1e-12
    cs = cand.sum()
    if cs <=0: return None
    cand /= cs
    return cand
```
In optimizer, evaluate candidate score with `_score_fast(cand,pad)`. If better, update best and cur.

```python
"""
Search for low-score nonnegative step-function heights.

The objective is score = 2*n*max(a*a) / sum(a)^2, where a*a is the
linear convolution.  The program initializes from explicit constructions,
especially coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2) and simple power laws, then runs
an FFT-accelerated power-mean / active-lag multiplicative search.  It also
does random block perturbations and finally verifies the best candidates
with the official evaluate_sequence() function before returning the best
sequence found.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def _as_nonnegative_float_array(seq):
    """Convert a sequence to a finite nonnegative float64 1D array, or None."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return None
    return arr


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    """Clip to nonnegative and scale to the requested L1 norm."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    a *= float(target) / s
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    return a


def _prefix_sequence(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2):
      a_k = binom(2k,k) / 4^k, with a_0 = 1.
    """
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        a[k] = a[k - 1] * float(2 * k - 1) / float(2 * k)
    return a


def _power_sequence(n, alpha):
    """Simple decreasing family a_k = (k+1)^(-alpha)."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    a = np.power(idx, -float(alpha))
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def _resample_nonnegative(arr, n):
    """Piecewise-linear resampling of a nonnegative sequence to length n."""
    arr = _as_nonnegative_float_array(arr)
    if arr is None:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    m = arr.size
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(arr[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return arr.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
    return np.clip(y, 0.0, 1000.0)


def _pad_length(n):
    """Power-of-two FFT padding long enough for convolution and cross grads."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return 1
    m = max(1, 3 * n - 3)
    return 1 << max(0, (m - 1).bit_length())


def _fast_conv(a, pad):
    """Real linear convolution a*a, truncated to the correct length."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, int(pad))
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, int(pad))[:2 * max(1, n - 1) + 1]
    # FFT roundoff can create tiny negatives.
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_fast(a, pad):
    """Fast score assuming L1(a)=1; for general a it is scale invariant enough here."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = _fast_conv(a, pad)
    m = float(c.max())
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(m) or not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _active_gradient(a, w, pad, n):
    """
    For weights w_k on lags, compute d_i = sum_k w_k * a_{k-i},
    which is one half of the subgradient of the weighted lag sum.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    ar = a[::-1]
    Ww = np.fft.rfft(w, int(pad))
    Wa = np.fft.rfft(ar, int(pad))
    cor = np.fft.irfft(Ww * Wa, int(pad))
    return cor[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def _block_perturb_candidate(best, n, pad, rng):
    """Return a random nonnegative block-scaled perturbation of best, or None."""
    try:
        cand = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            return None
        cand /= s

        width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n // 1200), max(3, n // 48)))
        if width <= 0:
            width = 1
        if width >= n:
            width = max(1, n // 2)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        amp = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.15)))
        cand[start:start + width] *= amp
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        cand += 1e-12
        cs = float(cand.sum())
        if cs <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(cs):
            return None
        cand /= cs
        return cand
    except Exception:
        return None


def _optimize_start(a0, n, time_limit, rng):
    """
    FFT power-mean / active-lag multiplicative descent from one start.
    Always keeps the best sequence seen so far under the fast evaluator.
    """
    a = _normalize(a0, 1.0)
    if a is None:
        a = _normalize(_prefix_sequence(n), 1.0)
    if a is None:
        a = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)

    n = int(a.size)
    pad = _pad_length(n)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = _score_fast(a, pad)
    cur = a.copy()

    eta = 0.25
    p = 2.0
    last_improve = 0
    it = 0
    end = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_limit))

    while time.monotonic() < end - 0.35:
        c = _fast_conv(cur, pad)
        score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            cur = best.copy()
            score = best_score

        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = cur.copy()
            last_improve = it
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.02)

        # If the walk wandered too far, pull it back to the best point.
        if score > best_score * 1.35:
            cur = best.copy()
            cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.04, size=n))
            cur = np.clip(cur, 0.0, 1000.0)
            cur += 1e-11
            cs = float(cur.sum())
            if cs > 0 and np.isfinite(cs):
                cur /= cs
            else:
                cur = best.copy()

        # Build active-lag weights from a smooth power of the convolution.
        logc = np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300))
        maxlog = float(logc.max())
        w = np.exp(p * (logc - maxlog))
        wsum = float(w.sum())
        if wsum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
            w = np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
        else:
            w /= wsum

        d = _active_gradient(cur, w, pad, n)
        dmin = float(d.min())
        dmax = float(d.max())
        denom = dmax - dmin

        if denom > 1e-15 and np.isfinite(denom):
            frac = 1.0 - eta * (d - dmin) / denom
            frac = np.clip(frac, 1e-8, None)
            new = cur * frac
        else:
            # Stationary-looking point: weekly random logarithmic jitter.
            new = cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, size=n))

        new = np.clip(new, 0.0, 1000.0)
        new += 1e-11
        ns = float(new.sum())
        if ns <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ns):
            new = cur.copy()
        else:
            new /= ns

        # Occasional smoothed Frank-Wolfe-style move toward low-gradient coords.
        if it % 25 == 20 and denom > 1e-15:
            z = (d - dmin) / denom
            b = np.exp(-z)
            bsum = float(b.sum())
            if bsum > 0.0 and np.isfinite(bsum):
                b /= bsum
                alpha_fw = 0.04 + 0.04 * min(1.0, eta)
                cand = (1.0 - alpha_fw) * new + alpha_fw * b
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
                cand += 1e-12
                cand_s = float(cand.sum())
                if cand_s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(cand_s):
                    cand /= cand_s
                    new = cand

        cur = new
        it += 1

        # Periodic random block search from the best point.
        if it % 220 == 80:
            for _ in range(4):
                cand = _block_perturb_candidate(best, n, pad, rng)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                cand_score = _score_fast(cand, pad)
                if cand_score < best_score:
                    best_score = cand_score
                    best = cand.copy()
                    cur = best.copy()
                    last_improve = it
                    break

        # Adaptive restarts.
        gap = it - last_improve
        if gap > 120:
            eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.95)
            p = min(64.0, p * 1.05)
        elif gap > 350:
            cur = best.copy()
            cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.09, size=n))
            cur = np.clip(cur, 0.0, 1000.0)
            cur += 1e-10
            cs = float(cur.sum())
            if cs > 0.0 and np.isfinite(cs):
                cur /= cs
            else:
                cur = best.copy()
            eta = 0.25
            p = 2.0
            last_improve = 0
        else:
            p = min(64.0, p * 1.002)

    return best, best_score


def _make_starts(n, rng):
    """Create a small set of diverse nonnegative initial sequences."""
    n = int(n)
    starts = []
    pref = _prefix_sequence(n)
    if pref.size == n:
        starts.append(pref)

    # Several power-law shapes.
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.5, 0.55):
        starts.append(_power_sequence(n, alpha))

    # Mix left and right singular endpoints.
    pref_rev = pref[::-1]
    for lam in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4):
        starts.append(pref + lam * pref_rev)

    # Random smooth multiplication of the prefix.
    if pref.size:
        r = pref.copy()
        r *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, size=n))
        starts.append(r)

    # Use the available historical construction if it exists and is usable.
    try:
        hist = _as_nonnegative_float_array(height_sequence_1)
        if hist is not None and hist.size > 0:
            if hist.size == n:
                starts.append(hist)
            elif hist.size > 8:
                starts.append(_resample_nonnegative(hist, n))
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Normalize and remove invalid entries.
    clean = []
    for s in starts:
        a = _normalize(s, 1.0)
        if a is None or a.size != n:
            continue
        clean.append(a)
        if len(clean) >= 8:
            break

    if not clean:
        clean.append(_normalize(pref, 1.0))
    return clean


def _optimize_length(n, time_limit, rng):
    """Run several starts/optimizer passes for one fixed length."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0 or time_limit <= 1.0:
        seq = _normalize(_prefix_sequence(max(1, n)), 1.0)
        if seq is None:
            seq = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        pad = _pad_length(seq.size)
        return seq, _score_fast(seq, pad)

    starts = _make_starts(n, rng)
    pad = _pad_length(n)

    scored = []
    for a in starts:
        sc = _score_fast(a, pad)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, a))

    if not scored:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        return a, _score_fast(a, pad)

    scored.sort()

    # Optimize only the best few starts, splitting time among them.
    top = scored[:3]
    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    start_time = time.monotonic()
    per = max(0.0, float(time_limit) / float(len(top)))

    for idx, (sc0, a0) in enumerate(top):
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_time
        remaining = time_limit - elapsed
        if remaining < 2.0:
            break
        # Give the first selected start a little more time.
        allow = remaining * (1.0 if idx == 0 else 0.55)
        if allow < 1.0:
            allow = remaining
        seq, sc = _optimize_start(a0, n, allow, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq

    if best_seq is None:
        seq = scored[0][1]
        best_score = scored[0][0]
        best_seq = seq

    return best_seq, _score_fast(best_seq, pad)


def _exact_score(seq_arr):
    """Call the official evaluator on a numpy array; return a float score."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq_arr, dtype=np.float64)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        lst = [float(x) for x in arr]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Generate a best-effort nonnegative sequence.

    The function runs within the allotted time and returns a Python list of
    finite nonnegative floats.  It favors sequences with very low official
    evaluate_sequence() score.
    """
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 5.0:
        return [1.0]

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    # Leave a small tail for exact verification and serializing.
    hard_deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget - 8.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    lengths = []
    try:
        plen = int(len(height_sequence_1))
        if 128 <= plen <= 12000:
            lengths.append(plen)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # A longer length is often closer to the asymptotic regime.
    if 8192 not in lengths:
        lengths.append(8192)

    # A medium length can escape any length-specific behavior and is cheap.
    if 1024 not in lengths:
        lengths.append(1024)

    # Keep the list short and in acceptable evaluation cost.
    clean_lengths = []
    for n in lengths:
        if 32 <= n <= 12000 and n not in clean_lengths:
            clean_lengths.append(int(n))
        if len(clean_lengths) >= 3:
            break
    if not clean_lengths:
        clean_lengths = [2048]

    # Allocate most of the main phase to the earlier lengths.
    main_deadline = t0 + max(0.0, (hard_deadline - t0) * 0.86)
    total_main = max(0.0, main_deadline - t0)

    if len(clean_lengths) == 1:
        fracs = [1.0]
    elif len(clean_lengths) == 2:
        fracs = [0.72, 0.28]
    else:
        fracs = [0.56, 0.32, 0.12]

    candidate_scores = []
    candidate_seqs = []

    now = time.monotonic()
    for idx, n in enumerate(clean_lengths):
        if now >= main_deadline - 2.0:
            break
        alloc = fracs[idx] * total_main
        remain = main_deadline - now
        t_allow = max(0.0, min(alloc, remain))
        if t_allow < 2.0:
            t_allow = remain

        seq, sc = _optimize_length(n, t_allow, rng)
        if seq is None:
            seq = _normalize(_prefix_sequence(n), 1.0)
        if seq is None:
            seq = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

        sc2 = _score_fast(seq, _pad_length(seq.size))
        if np.isfinite(sc2):
            candidate_scores.append(sc2)
            candidate_seqs.append(seq)

        # Always keep the clean analytical prefix for this length as a safe fallback.
        pref = _normalize(_prefix_sequence(n), 1.0)
        if pref is not None:
            psc = _score_fast(pref, _pad_length(n))
            if np.isfinite(psc):
                candidate_scores.append(psc)
                candidate_seqs.append(pref)

        now = time.monotonic()

    # Ensure at least one candidate exists.
    if not candidate_seqs:
        pref = _normalize(_prefix_sequence(2048), 1.0)
        if pref is None:
            pref = np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64)
        candidate_scores.append(_score_fast(pref, _pad_length(pref.size)))
        candidate_seqs.append(pref)

    records = [(float(candidate_scores[i]), i) for i in range(len(candidate_scores))]
    records.sort()

    best_exact_seq = None
    best_exact_score = float("inf")

    # Verify the best fast candidates with the official evaluator if time allows.
    verified = 0
    for sc, idx in records:
        if verified > 14:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 4.0:
            break
        seq = candidate_seqs[idx]
        exact = _exact_score(seq)
        verified += 1
        if np.isfinite(exact) and exact < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = exact
            best_exact_seq = seq

    # Fallback to the best fast candidate if no exact evaluation completed.
    if best_exact_seq is None:
        best_idx = records[0][1]
        best_exact_seq = candidate_seqs[best_idx]

    final = np.asarray(best_exact_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1000.0)
    final += 0.0
    out = [float(x) for x in final]

    try:
        final_score = best_exact_score
        if not np.isfinite(final_score):
            final_score = _score_fast(final, _pad_length(final.size))
        print("Length proposed:", len(out))
        print("Estimated score:", float(final_score))
    except Exception:
        pass

    return out
```
